not iid
when conditioned on
\theta_{1:K}
. Existing tools for statistical analysis can struggle in the setting without the appropriate averages of iid quantities. However, our analytical tools involving rate-distortion theory allow us to handle such problem instances with relative ease and produce reasonable upper bounds on error in learning settings involving sequences of data. In the following section, we extend these tools to analyze a more complex transformer data generating process.

7.3
Transformer Process

7.3.1
Data Generating Process

We now study a much more complex transformer data generating process over a vocabulary of size
d
. We are interested in estimating the random matrices
\theta^{(1:L)}=(A^{(1)},V^{(1)},\ldots,A^{(L)},V^{(L)})
which are the weights of the transformer across
L
layers. Let
X_{0}:\Omega\mapsto\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
be a categorical random variable with arbitrary pmf. Then, for all
t\geq 0
, let

X_{t+1}\sim{\rm Softmax}(f_{\theta^{(1:L)}}(X_{t:t-K+1})),

where
f_{\theta^{(1:L)}}
is the layer
L
transformer with weights
\theta^{(1:L)}
which we will detail shortly. Notably, we assume that
X_{t+1}
only depends on the history via the most recent length
K
context
X_{t:t-K+1}
.

We use
U_{t}^{(\ell)}
to denote the output of layer
\ell
of the transformer at time
t
. Beginning at the input, let

U_{t}^{(0)}=\begin{bmatrix}\phi_{t-K+1}&\phi_{t-K+2}&\ldots&\phi_{t}\end{bmatrix},

where
\phi_{t}=\Phi_{X_{t}}
for some known vectors
\Phi_{1},\ldots,\Phi_{d}\in\Re^{r}
with unit L2 norm. For
\ell>0
, let

U_{t}^{(\ell)}=\text{Clip}\left(V^{(\ell)}U_{t}^{(\ell-1)}\text{Attn}^{(\ell)}\left(U_{t}^{(\ell-1)}\right)\right),

where
\text{Clip}(A)
clips the columns of matrix
A
to have L2 norm at most 1 and

\text{Attn}^{(\ell)}\left(U_{t}^{(\ell-1)}\right)={\rm Softmax}\left(\frac{U^{(\ell-1)\top}_{t}A^{(\ell)}U_{t}^{(\ell-1)}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)

denotes the attention matrix of layer
(\ell)
where
{\rm Softmax}
denotes the softmax function applied elementwise along the columns. The matrix
A^{(\ell)}\in\Re^{r\times r}
can be interpreted as the product of the key and query matrices and without loss of generality, we assume that the elements of the matrices
A^{(\ell)}
are distributed iid
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
(Gaussian assumption is not crucial but unit variance is). The matrix
V^{(\ell)}
resembles the value matrix and we assume that the rows of
V^{(\ell)}
are distributed iid
{\rm Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
. For
\ell<L
, we have that
V^{(\ell)}\in\Re^{r\times r}
, whereas
V^{(L)}\in\Re^{d\times r}
.

Finally,
f_{\theta^{(1:L)}}(X_{t:t-K+1})=U_{t}^{(L)}[-1]
, which denotes the final column of the output matrix as is common in autoregressive generation with transformers.

7.3.2
Preliminary Results

In this section, we provide several Lemmas which culminate in a
T
-horizon rate-distortion upper bound for the multilayer transformer process. The techniques mirror those introduced in the analysis of the finite-width MLP process. Just as for the MLP process, we restrict our attention to approximations
\tilde{\theta}=(\tilde{\theta}^{(1)},\ldots,\tilde{\theta}^{(L)})
which are independent from each other. Under this assumptions, we can upper bound the
T
-horizon distortion in the following way:

Lemma 48
.

(
T
-horizon distortion upper bound)

For all
T,L\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
, if
\theta^{(i)}\perp\theta^{(j)}
,
\tilde{\theta}^{(i)}\perp\tilde{\theta}^{(j)}
, and
\theta^{(i)}\perp\tilde{\theta}^{(j)}
for
i\neq j
, then

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\tilde{P}_{t})\right]\ \leq\ \frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(H_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},X_{0})\|\mathbb{P}(H_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},U_{t}^{(\ell)}))\right]

Proof.

Fix arbitrary
t<T
. Recall that
\tilde{P}_{t}^{(\ell)}=\mathbb{P}(Y_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(\ell+1)},\tilde{\theta}^{(1:\ell)},H_{t})
is the prediction derived from approximating the first
\ell
layers with
\tilde{\theta}^{(1:\ell)}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\tilde{P}^{(\ell-1)}_{t}\|\tilde{P}^{(\ell)}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}(X_{t+1};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(1:\ell)},H_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\mathbb{I}(H_{t+1},\tilde{\theta}^{(1:\ell-1)};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)})-\mathbb{I}(H_{t},\tilde{\theta}^{(1:\ell-1)};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{I}(H_{t+1},\tilde{\theta}^{(1:\ell-1)};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},X_{0})

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\mathbb{I}(H_{t+1},\theta^{(1:\ell-1)};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},X_{0})

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}\mathbb{I}(H_{t+1},\theta^{(1:\ell-1)};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},X_{0})-\mathbb{I}(\theta^{(1:\ell-1)};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},X_{0})

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{=}\mathbb{I}(H_{t+1};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\theta^{(1:\ell-1)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},X_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(H_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},X_{0})\|\mathbb{P}(H_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},U^{(\ell)}_{0}))\right],

where
(a)
follows from the chain rule of mutual information,
(b)
follows from the independence assumptions,
(c)
follows from the data processing inequality applied to the markov chain
\theta_{i}\perp\tilde{\theta}^{(1:\ell-1)}|(H_{t+1},\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\theta^{(1:\ell-1)},X_{0}^{K})
,
(d)
follows from the fact that
\mathbb{I}(\theta^{(1:\ell-1)};\theta_{i}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}_{i},X_{0})=0
, and
(e)
follows from the chain rule of mutual information. The result follows by summing for all
t\in\{0,1,...,T-1\}
.
∎

The terms in the RHS quantify the expected prediction error incurred from approximating the weights at layer
\ell
with
\tilde{\theta}
. This approximation at layer
\ell
incurs deviation which will propagate to later layers of the network. The following result bounds this propagation via an upper bound on the squared Lipschitz constant of the transformer layers. Note that we use
\|\cdot\|_{\sigma}
to denote the
operator norm
of a matrix and
\|\cdot\|_{F}
to denote the
frobenius norm
.

Lemma 49
.

(transformer layer Lipschitz)

For all
d,r,K,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, if

\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}={\rm Clip}\left(V^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}{\rm Attn}^{(\ell)}\left(\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right)\right),

then

\left\|U^{(\ell)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}\right\|^{2}_{F}\ \leq\ 2K\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left(1+\frac{4K\|A^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}}{r}\right)\cdot\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right\|^{2}_{F}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\ \left\|U^{(\ell)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\|\text{Clip}\left(V^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right)-\text{Clip}\left(V^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\left\|V^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-V^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}_{k}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}2\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{K}2\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\sigma\left(\frac{\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}_{k}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\sum_{k=1}^{K}2\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{2K}{r}\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}_{k}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}2K\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right\|^{2}_{F}+\frac{4K}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}-U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}_{k}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4K}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}_{k}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)\top}A^{(\ell)}\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}_{k}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{\leq}2K\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right\|^{2}_{F}+\frac{4K^{2}}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\|A^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}_{k}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4K}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\|A^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle=2K\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right\|^{2}_{F}+\frac{8K^{2}}{r}\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\|A^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle=2K\|V^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left(1+\frac{4K\|A^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}}{r}\right)\cdot\left\|U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\right\|^{2}_{F},

where
(a)
follows from the fact that Clip is a contraction mapping, where in
(b)
,
U^{(\ell-1)}_{k}
denotes the
k
th column of
U^{(\ell-1)}\in\Re^{d\times K}
,
(c)
follows from the fact
\|\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\leq K
and the fact that softmax is
1
-Lipschitz,
(d)
follows from the fact that
(x+z)^{2}\leq 2(x-y)^{2}+2(y+z)^{2}
, and
(e)
follows from the fact that
\|U^{(\ell-1)}U^{(\ell-1)\top}\|_{\sigma}\leq K
.
∎

In the above result,
\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}
is the result of passing a perturbed input
\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}
through layer
\ell-1
of the transformer. The result upper bounds the squared frobenius norm of
U^{(\ell)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}
via the squared frobenius norm of the input perturbation
U^{(\ell-1)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}
. For our analysis, the perturbed input
\tilde{U}^{(\ell-1)}={\rm Clip}(\tilde{V}^{(\ell-1)}U^{(\ell-2)}\tilde{{\rm Attn}}^{(\ell-1)}(U^{(\ell-2)}))
, the result of passing the layer
\ell-2
output
U^{(\ell-2)}
through the approximation
(\tilde{A}^{(\ell-1)},\tilde{V}^{(\ell-1)})
. The following result bounds the expected squared frobenius norm of this perturbation.

Lemma 50
.

For all
d,r,K\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
and
\epsilon\geq 0
, if
V\in\Re^{r\times r}
consists of elements distributed iid
\mathcal{N}(0,1/r)
,
A\in\Re^{r\times r}
consists of elements distributed
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, for all
\ell\in[L]
,

\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}={\rm Clip}\left(\tilde{V}^{(\ell)}U^{(\ell-1)}\tilde{{\rm Attn}}^{(\ell)}\left(U^{(\ell-1)}\right)\right),

where for all
\ell<L
,
\mathbb{E}[\|V^{(\ell)}-\tilde{V}^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{F}]\leq\epsilon,\ \mathbb{E}[\|A^{(\ell)}-\tilde{A}^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{F}]\leq\epsilon
, and
\mathbb{E}[\|V^{(L)}-\tilde{V}^{(L)}\|^{2}_{F}]\leq\epsilon,\ \mathbb{E}[\|A^{(L)}-\tilde{A}^{(L)}\|^{2}_{F}]\leq r\epsilon/d
, then

\mathbb{E}\left[\|U^{(\ell)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}\|^{2}_{F}\right]\leq\begin{cases}2K^{2}\left(1+K\right)\epsilon&\text{ if }\ell<L\\
2K\left(1+K\right)\epsilon&\text{ if }\ell=L\\
\end{cases}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left\|U^{(\ell)}-\tilde{U}^{(\ell)}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\ \left\|V^{(\ell)}u{\rm Attn}^{(\ell)}(u)-\tilde{V}^{(\ell)}u\tilde{{\rm Attn}}^{(\ell)}(u)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|Vu\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}Au}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\tilde{V}u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|\left(V-\tilde{V}\right)u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|Vu\left(\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}Au}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|V-\tilde{V}\right\|^{2}_{F}\left\|u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|V\right\|^{2}_{F}\left\|u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}Au}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}2\epsilon\cdot\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\|u\|^{2}_{F}\cdot\left\|\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}+2\mathbb{E}\left[\|V\|^{2}_{F}\cdot\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\|u\|^{2}_{F}\left\|\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}Au}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}2\epsilon K^{2}+2\mathbb{E}\left[Kr\cdot\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|\frac{u^{\top}Au}{\sqrt{r}}-\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}u}{\sqrt{r}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{=}2\epsilon K^{2}+2K\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\left(u_{i}^{\top}(A-\tilde{A})u_{j}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon K^{2}+2K\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\left\|A-\tilde{A}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=2K^{2}\epsilon+2K^{3}\epsilon,

where
(a)
follows from the fact that
\|a+b\|^{2}_{F}\leq 2\|a\|^{2}_{F}+2\|b\|^{2}_{F}
for all matrices
a,b
,
(b)
follows from the fact that
\|ab\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|a\|^{2}_{\sigma}\|b\|^{2}_{F}
and
\|a\|^{2}_{\sigma}\leq\|a\|^{2}_{F}
for all matrices
a,b
,
(c)
follows from the fact that
\mathbb{E}\left[\|V-\tilde{V}\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\epsilon
,
(d)
follows from the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\|V\|^{2}_{F}]=r
, and the fact that softmax is
1
-Lipschitz, and where in
(e)
,
x_{i}
denotes the
i
th column of matrix
x
.

For
\ell=L
,

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left\|U^{(L)}-\tilde{U}^{(L)}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\ \left\|V^{(L)}u{\rm Attn}^{(L)}(u)[-1]-\tilde{V}^{(L)}u\tilde{{\rm Attn}}^{(L)}(u)[-1]\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|V^{(L)}u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}A^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\tilde{V}^{(L)}u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|\left(V^{(L)}-\tilde{V}^{(L)}\right)u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|V^{(L)}u\left(\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}A^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|V^{(L)}-\tilde{V}^{(L)}\right\|^{2}_{F}\left\|u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\ +2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|V^{(L)}\right\|^{2}_{F}\left\|u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}A^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-u\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}2\epsilon\cdot\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\|u\|^{2}_{F}\cdot\left\|\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle\ +2\mathbb{E}\left[\|V^{(L)}\|^{2}_{F}\cdot\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\|u\|^{2}_{F}\left\|\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}A^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)-\sigma\left(\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right)\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}2\epsilon K+2\mathbb{E}\left[Kd\cdot\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left\|\frac{u^{\top}A^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}-\frac{u^{\top}\tilde{A}^{(L)}u[-1]}{\sqrt{r}}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{=}2\epsilon K+\frac{2Kd}{r}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\left(u_{i}^{\top}(A^{(L)}-\tilde{A}^{(L)})u[-1]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon K+\frac{2Kd}{r}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{K}\left\|A^{(L)}-\tilde{A}^{(L)}\right\|^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=2K\epsilon+2K^{2}\epsilon,

where
(a)
follows from the fact that
\|a+b\|^{2}_{F}\leq 2\|a\|^{2}_{F}+2\|b\|^{2}_{F}
for all matrices
a,b
,
(b)
follows from the fact that
\|ab\|^{2}_{F}\leq\|a\|^{2}_{\sigma}\|b\|^{2}_{F}
and
\|a\|^{2}_{\sigma}\leq\|a\|^{2}_{F}
for all matrices
a,b
,
(c)
follows from the fact that
\mathbb{E}\left[\|V-\tilde{V}\|^{2}_{F}\right]=\epsilon
,
(d)
follows from the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\|V\|^{2}_{F}]=d
, and the fact that softmax is
1
-Lipschitz, and where in
(e)
,
u_{i}
denotes the
i
th column of matrix
u
.
∎

With these preliminary results in place, we present the following upper bound on the
T
-horizon distortion.

Lemma 51
.

(transformer
T
-horizon distortion upper bound)

For all
d,r,t,K,L\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, and
\ell\in[L]
, if

\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)}\ =\ (\tilde{V}^{(\ell)},\tilde{A}^{(\ell)})=(V^{(\ell)}+Z^{(\ell)},A^{(\ell)}+B^{(\ell)}),

where

(Z^{(\ell)},B^{(\ell)})\perp(V^{(\ell)},A^{(\ell)}),\quad Z^{(\ell)}\overset{iid}{\sim}\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon/r^{2})&\text{ if }\ell<L\\
\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon/(rd)&\text{ if }\ell=L\\
\end{cases};\quad B^{(\ell)}\overset{iid}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon/r^{2}),

then

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\tilde{P}_{t})\right]\ \leq\ \frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\epsilon KL(t+1)\left(8K(1+16K)\right)^{L}.

Proof.

For all
d,r,t,K,L\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
,
0\leq\epsilon
, and
\ell\in[L]
, let

\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)}\ =\ (\tilde{V}^{(\ell)},\tilde{A}^{(\ell)})=(V^{(\ell)}+Z^{(\ell)},A^{(\ell)}+B^{(\ell)}),

where

(Z^{(\ell)},B^{(\ell)})\perp(V^{(\ell)},A^{(\ell)}),\quad Z^{(\ell)}\overset{iid}{\sim}\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon/r^{2})&\text{ if }\ell<L\\
\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon/(rd)&\text{ if }\ell=L\\
\end{cases};\quad B^{(\ell)}\overset{iid}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon/r^{2}).

Then,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\tilde{P}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\ \mathbb{I}(X_{t+1};\theta^{(\ell)}|\tilde{\theta}^{(1:L)},\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},H_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\ \mathbb{I}(H_{t+1};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\theta^{(1:\ell-1)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},X_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{k=0}^{t}\ \mathbb{I}(X_{k+1};\theta^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\theta^{(1:\ell-1)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},H_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{k=0}^{t}\ \mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}(X_{k+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},H_{k})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X_{k+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L-1)},\tilde{\theta}^{(L)},H_{k})\right)\right]

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
48
. We now upper bound each term in the final equation above.

We begin with the base case
\ell=L
. For all
t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},H_{t})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L-1)},\tilde{\theta}^{(L)},H_{t})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},H_{t})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L-1)},\theta^{(L)}\leftarrow\tilde{\theta}^{(L)},H_{t})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|f_{\theta^{(1:L)}}(X_{t:t-K+1})-f_{\tilde{\theta}^{(L)}}\left(f_{\theta^{(1:L-1)}}(X_{t:t-K+1})\right)\right\|^{2}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}2K(1+K)\epsilon,

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
33
and
(b)
follows from Lemma
50
.

For
\ell<L
and for all
t
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},H_{t}\right)\ \|\ \mathbb{P}\left(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:\ell-1)},\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},H_{t}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},H_{t}\right)\ \|\ \mathbb{P}\left(X_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:\ell-1)},\theta^{(\ell+1:L)},\theta^{(\ell)}\leftarrow\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)},H_{t}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|f_{\theta^{(1:L)}}(X_{t:t-K+1})-\left(f_{\theta^{(\ell+1:L)}}\circ f_{\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)}}\circ f_{\theta^{(1:\ell-1)}}\right)(X_{t:t-K+1})\right\|^{2}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[2K\|V^{(L)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left(1+\frac{4K\|A^{(L)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}}{r}\right)\cdot\left\|f_{\theta^{(1:L-1)}}(X_{t:t-K+1})-\left(f_{\theta^{(\ell+1:L-1)}}\circ f_{\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)}}\circ f_{\theta^{(1:\ell-1)}}\right)(X_{t:t-K+1})\right\|^{2}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\prod_{l=\ell+1}^{L}2K\|V^{(l)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left(1+\frac{4K\|A^{(l)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}}{r}\right)\right)\cdot\left\|f_{\theta^{(1:\ell)}}(X_{t:t-K+1})-\left(f_{\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)}}\circ f_{\theta^{(1:\ell-1)}}\right)(X_{t:t-K+1})\right\|^{2}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\prod_{l=\ell+1}^{L}2K\|V^{(l)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\left(1+\frac{4K\|A^{(l)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}}{r}\right)\right)\cdot 2K^{2}(1+K)\epsilon\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(f)}{\leq}\left(8K(1+16K)\right)^{L-\ell}\cdot 2K^{2}(1+K)\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon K\left(8K(1+16K)\right)^{L-\ell+1}

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
33
,
(b)
follows from Lemma
32
,
(c)
and
(d)
follow from Lemma
49
,
(e)
follows from Lemma
50
and the fact that
(V^{(i)},A^{(i)}\perp(V^{(j)},A^{(j)}))
and
(V^{(i)},A^{(i)}\perp(\tilde{V}^{(j)},\tilde{A}^{(j)}))
for
i\neq j
, and
(f)
follows from the fact that
A^{(L)}\perp V^{(L)}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\|A^{(L)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\right]\leq 4r
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\|V^{(L)}\|^{2}_{\sigma}\right]\leq 4
.

As a result,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\tilde{P}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{k=0}^{t}\ \mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}(X_{k+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L)},H_{k})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X_{k+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{(1:L-1)},\tilde{\theta}^{(L)},H_{k})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{k=0}^{t}\ \epsilon K\left(8K(1+16K)\right)^{L-\ell+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\epsilon KL(t+1)\left(8K(1+16K)\right)^{L-\ell+1}.

∎

We conclude this section with an upper bound for the
T
-horizon rate-distortion upper bound.

Lemma 52
.

(transformer
T
-horizon rate-distortion upper bound)

For all
d,r,t,K,L\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, if for all
t
,
X_{t}
is generated by the transformer process, then

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta^{(1:L)})\ \leq\ r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(1+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{\epsilon}\right).

Proof.

For all
\ell\in[L]
, let

\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)}\ =\ (\tilde{V}^{(\ell)},\tilde{A}^{(\ell)})=(V^{(\ell)}+Z^{(\ell)},A^{(\ell)}+B^{(\ell)}),

where

(Z^{(\ell)},B^{(\ell)})\perp(V^{(\ell)},A^{(\ell)}),\quad Z^{(\ell)}\overset{iid}{\sim}\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}/r^{2})&\text{ if }\ell<L\\
\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}/(rd)&\text{ if }\ell=L\\
\end{cases};\quad B^{(\ell)}\overset{iid}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}/r^{2}).

Let
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}(\theta^{(1:L)};\tilde{\theta}^{(1:L)})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\theta}^{(1:L)})-\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\theta}^{(1:L)}|\theta^{(1:L)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)})-\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\theta}^{(\ell)}|\theta^{(\ell)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\mathbf{h}(\tilde{V}^{(\ell)})-\mathbf{h}(\tilde{V}^{(\ell)}|V^{(\ell)})+\mathbf{h}(\tilde{A}^{(\ell)})-\mathbf{h}(\tilde{A}^{(\ell)}|A^{(\ell)})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{r^{2}}\right)\right)-\frac{r^{2}}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{r^{2}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{rd}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{rd}\right)\right)-\frac{rd}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{rd}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\left(1+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{r^{2}}\right)\right)-\frac{r^{2}}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{r^{2}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Lr\cdot\max\{r,d\}}{2}\ln\left(1+\frac{\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L})}{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{Lr^{2}}{2}\ln\left(1+\frac{r^{2}KLT(8K(1+16K)))^{L}}{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Lr\cdot\max\{r,d\}\ln\left(1+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{\epsilon}\right),

We now verify that the distortion is
\leq\epsilon.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\tilde{P}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{I}(X_{t+1};\theta^{(1:L)}|\tilde{\theta}^{(1:L)},H_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\epsilon^{\prime}KL(t+1)\left(8K(1+16K)\right)^{L}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\prime}KLT\left(8K(1+16K)\right)^{L}

\displaystyle=\epsilon,

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
51
.
∎

7.3.3
Main Result

We now present an upper bound on the optimal expected error when the data is generated by the transformer process.

Theorem 53
.

(transformer error upper bound)

For all
d,r,L,K,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, if for all
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
X_{t}
is generated according to the transformer process, then

\mathcal{L}_{T}\ \leq\ \frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L^{2}\ln(8eK(1+16K))}{T}+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(\frac{2KT^{2}}{L}\right)}{T}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{T}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta^{(1:L)})}{T}+\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(1+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{\epsilon}\right)}{T}+\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(1+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L^{2}}{T}}\right)}{T}+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L^{2}}{T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(\frac{2KT^{2}(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{L}\right)}{T}+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L^{2}}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L^{2}\ln\left(8eK(1+16K)\right)}{T}+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(\frac{2KT^{2}}{L}\right)}{T},

where
(a)
follows from Theorem
17
,
(b)
follows from Lemma
52
,
(c)
follows by setting
\epsilon=r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L^{2}/T

∎

Notably, the result scales linearly in the product of the parameter count of the transformer and the depth of the transformer. Unlike in the MLP setting, we are unable to eliminate the quadratic depth dependence. This is due to the fact that it is unknown whether softmax attention obeys the condition that the expected squared lipschitz constant is
\leq 1
. In this work we upper bounded this lipschitz value by
(2K+8K^{2})
which results in the additional dependence on depth.

Summary

•

In this section, we demonstrate that the analytic tools which we developed seamlessly transfer to the setting in which data is no longer iid under some unknown distribution. To demonstrate concrete use of our tools, we derive error upper bounds for 2 settings involving learning from sequential data: 1) a simple binary AR(K) process, 2) a transformer process.

•

(binary AR(K) error upper bound)

For all embedding dimensions
d
and context lengths
K
, if for all
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},X_{t}
is generated according to the binary AR(K) process, then for all
T
,

\mathcal{L}_{T}\ \leq\ \frac{dK}{2T}\left(1+\ln\left(1+\frac{T}{4dK}\right)\right).

•

Consider a sequence which is generated by a transformer with vocabulary size
d
, embedding dimension
r
, depth
L
, and context length
K
. The following result holds:

•

(transformer error upper bound)

For all vocabulary sizes
d
, embedding dimensions
r
, depths
L
, and context lengths
K
, if for all
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
X_{t}
is generated according to the transformer process, then for all
T
,

\mathcal{L}_{T}\ \leq\ \frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L^{2}\ln(8eK(1+16K))}{T}+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(\frac{2KT^{2}}{L}\right)}{T}.

8
Meta-Learning

Figure 7
:
The above diagram depicts the pre-training of large language models as a meta-learning problem. The meta-parameters
\psi
specify a distribution over document types (tasks). Each document type (task)
\theta_{m}
specifies an autoregressive random process. Each document
(X^{(m)}_{0},X^{(m)}_{1},X^{(m)}_{2},\ldots)
, is represented by a sequence of tokens which is distributed according to the random process designated by the document type.

8.1
Data Generating Process

We now consider data generating processes with hierarchical structure. There are
M
data sequences, which we intuitively associate with
tasks
. For each task
m\in[M]
, we denote the sequence by
(X_{0}^{(m)},X_{1}^{(m)},\ldots)
. Each
m
th sequence is parameterized by random variable
\theta_{m}
. Conditioned on
\psi
,
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{M}
are iid.

The variable
\psi
, which we refer to as the
meta-parameters
, represents the information that is shared
across
the models. Conditioned on
\psi
,
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\ldots)
is iid. Hence, there is no further information shared between models beyond that present in
\psi
.

As a concrete example,
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\ldots,\theta_{M}
could be the parameters of language models, each specialized to produce text for different tasks across diverse domain such as medicine, code, mathematics, law, etc. And
\psi
could encode the frequencies at which different document types appear in the text corpus used for training. Then,
(X^{(m)}_{0},X^{(m)}_{1},X^{(m)}_{2},\ldots)
could be text tokens produced by a model parameterized by
\theta_{m}
.

8.2
Meta-Learning Error

We define notions of error for meta-learning, which directly parallel the definitions of Section
3.4
. We consider learning under a setting for which there exist
M
tasks and
T
observations per task. A learning algorithm produces for each
(m,t)\in[M]\times[T]
, a predictive distribution
P_{m,t}
of
X_{t+1}^{(m)}
after observing the concatenated history which we denote by

H_{m,t}=\left(X^{(1)}_{0:T},X^{(2)}_{0:T},\ldots,X^{(m-1)}_{0:T},X^{(m)}_{0:t}\right).

H_{m,t}
consists of all observations from tasks
1,\ldots,m-1
and up to the
t
-th observation of task
m
. We express our meta-learning algorithm in terms of a function
\pi
for which
P_{m,t}=\pi(H_{m,t})
. For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, we measure the error realized by our predictions
P_{m,t}
with respect to the gold-standard clairvoyant prediction
P^{*}_{m,t}=\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta,\psi,H_{m,t})
via the expected KL divergence:

\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(P^{*}_{m,t}\|P_{m,t}\right)\right].

8.3
Optimal Achievable Error

For all
(M,T)
, what
\pi
minimizes error? The following result states that the optimal algorithm assigns for all
(m,t)
,
P_{m,t}=\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|H_{m,t})
. We will denote this
posterior predictive distribution
as
\hat{P}_{m,t}
.

Lemma 54
.

(posterior predictive distribution is optimal)

For all
m,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]=\min_{\pi}\ \mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|P_{m,t})\right].

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|P_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\hat{P}_{m,t}(X^{(m)}_{t+1})}{P_{m,t}(X^{(m)}_{t+1})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\hat{P}_{m,t}(X^{(m)}_{t+1})}{P_{m,t}(X^{(m)}_{t+1})}\bigg|H_{m,t}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\hat{P}_{m,t}\|P_{m,t})\right]

The result follows from the fact that KL divergence is non-negative.
∎

Going forward, we will restrict our analysis to
\hat{P}_{m,t}
. We will denote this error as:

\mathcal{L}_{M,T}=\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right].

In the following section, we will establish general results which bound error via the total number of observations
M\cdot T
.

8.4
General Theoretical Results

We begin with a general result which expresses error in terms of two interpretable quantities.

Theorem 55
.

(error decomposition)

For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{M,T}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|H_{m,t}))\right]}_{{\rm meta\ error}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta_{m},\psi,H_{m,t})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t}))\right]}_{{\rm intra-task\ error}}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\ \mathbb{I}(X_{t+1}^{(m)};\psi,\theta_{m}|H_{m,t})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\psi,\theta_{m}|H_{m-1,T})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\psi|H_{m-1,T})+\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\psi,H_{m-1,T})

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\psi|H_{m-1,T})+\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\psi)

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi)}{MT}+\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\psi)}{MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|H_{m,t}))\right]}_{{\rm meta\ error}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta_{m},\psi,H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t}))\right]}_{{\rm intra-task\ error}},

where
(a),(b)
, and
(d)
follow from the chain rule of mutual information, and
(c)
follows from the meta-learning conditional independence assumptions.
∎

The meta error represents error which is attributed to learning about the meta-parameters
\psi
. The second term consists of the error which is incurred from learning about the task specific parameters
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{M}

conditioned
on
\psi
. This error encompasses the remaining information to be extracted for a particular task beyond what is present in
\psi
.

While this result is useful for conceptual understanding, it is abstract and we require further tools to produce more concrete bounds for specific model classes. We again leverage rate-distortion theory to establish suitable error bounds. Concretely, we define two rate-distortion functions. The first is the
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion function for
\psi
. First let
\tilde{\Psi}
denote the set of random variables
\tilde{\psi}
such that for all
m,t
,
X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{\psi}|(\psi,H_{m,t})
. Then, let

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)\ =\ \inf_{\tilde{\psi}\in\tilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon,M,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\psi;\tilde{\psi}),

where

\tilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon,M,T}\ =\ \left\{\tilde{\psi}\in\tilde{\Psi}:\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\psi},H_{m,t}))\right]\leq\epsilon\right\}.

For the intra-task parameters, let
\tilde{\Theta}_{m}
denote the set of random variables
\tilde{\theta}_{m}
such that for all
t
,
X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{\theta}_{m}|(\theta_{m},H_{m,t})
. Then, let

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)\ =\ \inf_{\tilde{\theta}_{m}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,m,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\theta_{m};\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi),

where

\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,m,T}\ =\ \left\{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{m}:\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta},\psi)}{T}\leq\epsilon\right\}.

With these definitions in place, we establish error bounds in terms of the above
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion functions.

Theorem 56
.

(
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion error bound)

For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and
m\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{M,T}

\displaystyle\ \leq\ \inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\left(\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)}{MT}+\epsilon\right)+\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\left(\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)}{T}+\epsilon\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{M,T}

\displaystyle\ \geq\ \sup_{\epsilon\geq 0}\min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)}{MT},\epsilon\right\}\quad+\quad\sup_{\epsilon\geq 0}\min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)}{T},\epsilon\right\}.

Proof.

We begin by showing the upper bound:

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}\left(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:m}\right)}{MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi)}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m},\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi|\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m},\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{\mathbb{I}(\psi;\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi|\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m},\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(\psi;\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi|\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi)}{T}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta}_{m},\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{\mathbb{I}(\psi;\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi|\tilde{\psi})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(\theta_{m};\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi)}{T}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta}_{m},\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)}{MT}+\epsilon+\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon^{\prime},M,T}(\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi)}{T}+\epsilon^{\prime},

where
(a)
and
(b)
follow from the data processing inequality and
(c)
follows from the definition of the rate-distortion functions. The upper bound follows from the fact that inequality
(c)
holds for all nonnegative
\epsilon
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
.

We now prove the lower bound.
Suppose that
\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi)<\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)

Let
\tilde{\psi}=\tilde{H}_{M,T}\notin\tilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon,M,T}
where
\tilde{H}_{M,T}
is another history sampled in the same manner as
H_{M,T}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{t+1};\psi|H_{m,t})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{t+1};\psi|\tilde{H}_{M,T},H_{m,t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{t+1};\psi|\tilde{\psi},X^{(m)}_{1},\ldots,X^{(m)}_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}\epsilon MT,

where
(a)
follows from the fact that conditioning reduces entropy and that
X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{H}_{M,T}|(\psi,H_{m,t})
and
(b)
follows from the fact that
\tilde{\psi}\notin\tilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon,M,T}
. Therefore, for all
\epsilon\geq 0
,
\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi)\geq\min\{H_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi),\epsilon MT\}
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{I}(H_{T}^{(m)};\theta_{m}|\psi)<\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)
. Let
\tilde{\theta}_{m}=\tilde{D}_{m}\notin\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,T}
where
\tilde{D}_{m}
is another history sampled in the same manner as
X^{(m)}_{0:T}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\psi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{t+1};\theta_{m}|X^{(m)}_{1},\ldots,X^{(m)}_{t},\psi)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{t+1};\theta_{m}|\tilde{D}_{m},X^{(m)}_{1},\ldots,X^{(m)}_{t},\psi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{t+1};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta}_{m},H_{m,t},\psi)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}\epsilon T,

where
(a)
follows from the fact that conditioning reduces entropy and that
X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{D}_{m}|(\psi,X^{(m)}_{1},\ldots,X^{(m)}_{t})
and
(b)
follows from the fact that
\tilde{\theta}_{m}\notin\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,T}
. Therefore, for all
\epsilon\geq 0
,
\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\psi)\geq\min\{H_{\epsilon,M,T}(\theta_{m}),\epsilon T\}
. The lower bound follows as a result.
∎

Theorem
56
establishes an intimate connection between the error of meta learning and the aforementioned rate-distortion functions via upper and lower bounds. This result will allow us to derive concrete error bounds for various meta-learning problems. In the subsequent sections, we will study two such problems. The first is a simple linear representation learning problem which is provided as exposition to acclimate the reader to the styles of analysis required for meta-learning. The second setting involves a mixture of transformers which resembles LLM pre-training.

8.5
Linear Representation Learning

Figure 8
:
The above diagram depicts the linear representation learning problem. The matrix
\psi\in\Re^{d\times r}
represents the information which is shared across the tasks. The intra-task information
\theta_{m}
is the product
\psi\xi^{(m)}
, where
\xi^{(m)}\in\Re^{r}
. The resulting vector
\theta_{m}
is passed through a softmax to produce a categorical distribution over
d
classes. Each corresponding sequence
X^{(m)}_{0},X^{(m)}_{1},X^{(m)}_{2},\ldots
is produced via iid sampling of the aforementioned categorical distribution.

8.5.1
Data Generating Process

We introduce a simple linear representation learning problem as a concrete example of meta-learning to demonstrate our method of analysis. In this example, the intra-task observations are iid, but we begin with such an example for simplicity and to demonstrate this as a special case of general meta-learning under our framework.

For all
d,r\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, we let
\psi:\Omega\mapsto\Re^{d\times r}
be distributed uniformly over the set of
d\times r
matrices with orthonormal columns. We assume that
d\gg r
. For all
m
, let
\xi^{(m)}:\Omega\mapsto\Re^{r}
be distributed iid
\mathcal{N}(0,I_{r}/r)
. We let
\theta_{m}=\psi\xi^{(m)}
. For each
(m,t)
, let
X_{t+1}^{(m)}
be drawn according to the following probability law:

X^{(m)}_{t+1}=\begin{cases}1&\text{w.p. }\sigma(\theta_{m})_{1}\\
2&\text{w.p. }\sigma(\theta_{m})_{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
d&\text{w.p. }\sigma(\theta_{m})_{d}\\
\end{cases},

where
\sigma(\theta_{m})_{j}=e^{\theta_{m,j}}/\sum_{k=1}^{d}e^{\theta_{m,k}}
. For each task
m
, the algorithm is tasked with estimating a vector
\theta_{m}
from sampled observations
(X_{1}^{(m)},X_{2}^{(m)},\ldots)
. By reasoning about data from previous tasks, the algorithm can estimate
\psi
, which reduces the burden of estimating
\theta_{m}
to just estimating
\xi^{(m)}
for each task. This is significant given the assumption that
d\gg r
.

8.5.2
Preliminary Results

We begin by establishing several lemmas which streamline our analysis. We begin with the following upper bound on the rate-distortion function for the meta parameter.

Lemma 57
.

(meta parameter rate-distortion upper bound)

For all
d,r,M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, if for all
(m,t)\in[M]\times[T]
,
X_{t}^{(m)}
is generated according to the linear representation learning process, then

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)\ \leq\ \frac{dr}{2MT}\ln\left(1+\frac{d}{r\left(e^{\frac{2\epsilon T}{r}}-1\right)}\right).

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\psi}=\psi+Z
where
Z\in\Re^{d\times r}
is independent of
\psi
and consists of elements which are distributed iid
\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{\prime})
where
\epsilon^{\prime}=(e^{\frac{2\epsilon T}{r}}-1)/d

We begin by upper bounding the rate.

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{I}(\psi;\tilde{\psi})}{MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\psi})-\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\psi}|\psi)}{MT}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{\frac{dr}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\left(\epsilon^{\prime}+\frac{1}{r}\right)\right)-\frac{dr}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e\epsilon^{\prime}\right)}{MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{dr\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{r\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)}{2MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{dr}{2MT}\ln\left(1+\frac{d}{r\left(e^{\frac{2\epsilon T}{r}}-1\right)}\right),

where
(a)
follows from the maximum differential entropy of a random variable of fixed variance being upper bounded by a Gaussian random variable.

We now upper bound the distortion. Let
V\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
and let
V\perp\psi
. Then,

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X_{t+1}^{(m)}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X_{t+1}^{(m)}\in\cdot|\tilde{\psi},H_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi|\tilde{\psi})}{MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T}|\tilde{\psi})-\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T}|\psi)}{MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{H}(X_{0:T}^{(m)}|\tilde{\psi},H_{m-1,T})-\mathbb{H}(X_{0:T}^{(m)}|\psi,H_{m-1,T})}{MT}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{H}(X_{0:T}^{(1)}|\tilde{\psi})-\mathbb{H}(X_{0:T}^{(1)}|\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(X_{0:T}^{(1)};\psi|\tilde{\psi})}{T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{I}(\theta_{1};\psi|\tilde{\psi})}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(\theta_{1}\in\cdot|\psi)\|\mathbb{P}(\theta_{1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\psi}))\right]}{T}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(\theta_{1}\in\cdot|\psi)\|\mathbb{P}(\theta_{1}\in\cdot|\psi\leftarrow\tilde{\psi}))\right]}{T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}(\theta_{1}+\sqrt{\delta}\cdot V\in\cdot|\psi)\|\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}(\theta_{1}+\sqrt{\delta}\cdot V|\psi\leftarrow\tilde{\psi}))\right]}{T}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\left|\delta I_{d}+\frac{\tilde{\psi}\tilde{\psi}^{\top}}{r}\right|}{\left|\delta I_{d}+\frac{\psi\psi^{\top}}{r}\right|}\right)-d+{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\delta I_{d}+\frac{\tilde{\psi}\tilde{\psi}^{\top}}{r}\right)^{-1}\left(\delta I_{d}+\frac{\psi\psi^{\top}}{r}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\left|\delta I_{d}+\frac{\tilde{\pi}\tilde{\psi}^{\top}}{r}\right|}{\left|\delta I_{d}+\frac{\psi\psi^{\top}}{r}\right|}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\left|\delta I_{d}\right|\cdot\left|I_{r}+\frac{\tilde{\psi}^{\top}\tilde{\psi}}{r\delta}\right|}{\left|\delta I_{d}\right|\cdot\left|I_{r}+\frac{\psi^{\top}\psi}{r\delta}\right|}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\left|I_{r}+\frac{\tilde{\psi}^{\top}\tilde{\psi}}{r\delta}\right|}{\left|I_{r}+\frac{I_{r}}{r\delta}\right|}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{\leq}\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left(\frac{\left|I_{r}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\psi}^{\top}\tilde{\psi}\right]}{r\delta}\right|}{\left|I_{r}+\frac{I_{r}}{r\delta}\right|}\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left(\frac{\left|I_{r}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[I_{r}+d\epsilon^{\prime}I_{r}\right]}{r\delta}\right|}{\left|I_{r}+\frac{I_{r}}{r\delta}\right|}\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{r}{2T}\ln\left(\frac{1+\frac{1+d\epsilon^{\prime}}{r\delta}}{1+\frac{1}{r\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{2T}\ln\left(1+d\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\epsilon,

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
33
,
(b)
follows from continuity of the KL divergence between two multivariate normal distributions w.r.t the covariance matrix,
(c)
follows from the fact that the trace term is upper bounded by
d
,
(d)
follows from the matrix determinant lemma,

\epsilon=\frac{1}{m}
, and
(e)
follows from Jensen’s inequality. The result follows.
∎

Recall that for meta-learning, the total estimation error is upper bounded by the sum of rate-distortion functions for the meta parameter and individual tasks. The following result establishes an upper bound on this intra-task rate-distortion function.

Lemma 58
.

(intra-task rate-distortion upper bound)

For all
r,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
,

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)\ \leq\ \frac{r}{2}\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right).

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\xi}=\xi^{(m)}+Z
where
Z\perp\xi^{(m)}
and
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon I_{r}/r)
. Let
\tilde{\theta}=\psi\tilde{\xi}
. We begin by upper bounding the rate.

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}(\theta_{m};\tilde{\theta}|\psi)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}(\xi;\tilde{\xi}|\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\xi}|\psi)-\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\xi}|\psi,\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\xi})-\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\xi}|\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{h}(\tilde{\xi})-\mathbf{h}(Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{2}\ln\left(2\pi e(\frac{\epsilon}{r}+\frac{1}{r})\right)-\frac{r}{2}\ln\left(\frac{2\pi e\epsilon}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{2}\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right).

We now upper bound the distortion:

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta_{m},\psi,H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\theta},\psi,H_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta},\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta},\psi)}{T}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}(X_{1}^{(m)};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(X^{(m)}_{1}\in\cdot|\theta_{m}\right)\|\mathbb{P}\left(X^{(m)}_{1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\theta}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(X^{(m)}_{1}\in\cdot|\theta_{m}\right)\|\mathbb{P}\left(X^{(m)}_{1}\in\cdot|\theta_{m}\leftarrow\tilde{\theta}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{\theta}-\theta_{m}\|^{2}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(m)}-\tilde{\xi}\right)^{\top}\psi^{\top}\psi\left(\xi^{(m)}-\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(m)}-\tilde{\xi}\right)^{\top}\left(\xi^{(m)}-\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{\top}Z\right]

\displaystyle=\epsilon

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
3
, and
(b)
follows from Lemma
32
.
∎

8.5.3
Main Result

With the rate-distortion upper bounds established from the previous section, we present the following result which upper bounds the error of linear representation learning.

Theorem 59
.

(linear representation learning error bound)

For all
d,r,M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, if for all
(m,t)
,
X^{(m)}_{t}
is generated according to the linear representation learning process, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{M,T}

\displaystyle\ \leq\ \frac{dr\ln\left(e\left(1+\frac{M}{r}\right)\right)}{2MT}+\frac{r\ln\left(e\left(1+\frac{2T}{r}\right)\right)}{2T}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \frac{\mathbb{H}_{M,T\epsilon}(\psi)}{MT}+\epsilon+\inf_{\epsilon^{\prime}\geq 0}\ \frac{\mathbb{H}_{T,\epsilon^{\prime}}(\theta_{m}|\psi)}{T}+\epsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \frac{dr}{2MT}\ln\left(1+\frac{d}{r\left(e^{\frac{2\epsilon T}{r}}-1\right)}\right)+\epsilon+\inf_{\epsilon^{\prime}\geq 0}\frac{r}{2n}\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{r\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)+\epsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{dr}{2MT}\ln\left(1+\frac{d}{r\left(e^{\frac{d\epsilon}{M}}-1\right)}\right)+\frac{dr}{2MT}+\frac{r\ln(1+\frac{2T}{r})}{2T}+\frac{r}{2T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{dr\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{r}\right)}{2MT}+\frac{dr}{2MT}+\frac{r\ln(1+\frac{2T}{r})}{2T}+\frac{r}{2T}

\displaystyle=\frac{dr\ln\left(e\left(1+\frac{M}{r}\right)\right)}{2MT}+\frac{r\ln\left(e\left(1+\frac{2T}{r}\right)\right)}{2T},

where
(a)
follows from Theorem
55
,

(b)
follows directly from Lemmas
57
and
58
, and
(c)
follows from setting
\epsilon=\frac{dr}{2MT}
and
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{r}{2T}
.
∎

The first term describes the error which is incurred in the process of estimating the meta-parameters
\psi
. Notably, this term is linear in
dr
, the parameter count of
\psi
, and decays linearly in
MT
, the total number of observations. This is both intuitive and desirable since each observation ought to provide information about
\psi
. Meanwhile, the second term describes the error which is incurred in the process of estimating the intra-task parameters
\theta_{m}
conditioned on
\psi
. In our problem instance,
\theta=\psi\xi
for
\xi\in\Re^{r}
, so the remaining uncertainty in
\theta
when conditioning on
\psi
is simply
\xi
. Therefore, the intra-task error is linear in
r
, the parameter count of
\xi
, and decays linearly in
T
, the number of observations pertinent to task
m
.

In the following section, we will characterize the learning performance under a much more complex process which resembles LLM pre-training

8.6
Mixture of Transformers

Figure 9
:
The above diagram depicts the mixture of transformers problem.
\psi
designates a mixture of many transformer models, each which is responsible for producing documents of a particular type. Each document is generated by sampling a transformer model
\theta_{m}
from the mixture
\psi
, and generating a sequence
(X_{0}^{(m)},X_{1}^{(m)},X_{2}^{(m)},\ldots)
autoregressively.

8.6.1
Data Generating Process

We consider a data generating process that produces
M
documents. For each
m
th document, we denote its sequence of tokens by
(X^{(m)}_{1},\ldots,X^{(m)}_{T})
. Each token takes value in
[d]
, where
d
is the size of the vocabulary. Each of the
d
token types is assigned a
known
embedding vector, which we denote as
\Phi_{j}
for
j\in[d]
. We assume that, for all
j
,
\|\Phi_{j}\|_{2}=1
. As shorthand, we let
\phi_{t}^{(m)}=\Phi_{X^{(m)}_{t}}
; this is the embedding associated with token
X^{(m)}_{t}
.

Each document is generated by a transformer model which is sampled iid from an unknown (random) categorical distribution
\alpha
. There are
N
categories, each corresponding to one transformer model. The distribution
\alpha
is sampled according to
\mathbb{P}(\alpha\in\cdot)=\text{Dirichlet}(N,[R/N,\ldots,R/N])
, with scale parameter
R\ll N
.

For each of the transformer models, let
K
denote the context length,
L
the depth, and
r
the attention dimensions. For all
i\in[n]
, let
\psi_{i}
denote the parameters of the
i
th transformer model and let
\psi=(\alpha,\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{N})
.

For all
t
,
X^{(m)}_{t+1}
is generated by transformer model
i_{m}
, where this index is sampled for document
m
. Hence,
X^{(m)}_{t+1}
is generated by a transformer model with weights
\psi_{i_{m}}
, given context
X^{(m)}_{t-K+1},\ldots,X^{(m)}_{t}
. We use
U^{(m)}_{t,\ell}
to denote the output of layer
\ell
at time
t
for document
m
. For all
m,t
, we let
(U^{(m)}_{t,0}=\phi_{t-K+1:t})
be the embeddings associated with the past
K
tokens. For
\ell>0
, we let
U^{(m)}_{t,\ell}
denote the output of layer
\ell
of the transformer with input
U^{(m)}_{t,0}
. For all
t\leq T,i<L,m\leq M
, let

\text{Attn}^{(\ell)}\left(U^{(m)}_{t,\ell-1}\right)={\rm Softmax}\left(\frac{U^{(m)\top}_{t,\ell-1}A^{(m)}_{\ell}U^{(m)}_{t,\ell-1}}{\sqrt{r}}\right)

denote the attention matrix of layer
\ell
for document
m
where the softmax function is applied to each column. The matrix
A^{(m)}_{\ell}\in\Re^{r\times r}
can be interpreted as the product of the key and query matrices and we assume that the elements of the matrices
A^{(m)}_{\ell}
are distributed iid
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Note that these weights belong to the transformer with parameters
\psi_{i_{m}}
.

Subsequently, we let

U^{(m)}_{t,\ell}=\text{Clip}\left(V^{(m)}_{\ell}U^{(m)}_{t,\ell-1}\text{Attn}^{(\ell)}\left(U^{(m)}_{t,\ell-1}\right)\right),

where Clip ensures that each column of the matrix input has
L_{2}
norm at most
1
. The matrix
V^{(m)}_{\ell}
resembles the value matrix and we assume that the elements of
V^{(m)}_{\ell}
are distributed iid
\mathcal{N}(0,1/d)
.

Finally, the next token is generated via sampling from the softmax of the final layer:

X^{(m)}_{t+1}\sim{\rm Softmax}\left(U^{(m)}_{t,L}[-1]\right),

where
U^{(m)}_{t,L}[-1]
denotes the right-most column of
U^{(m)}_{t,L}
. At each layer
\ell
, the parameters consist of the matrices
A^{(m)}_{\ell},V^{(m)}_{\ell}
. Therefore,
\theta_{m}=(i_{m},\psi_{i_{m}})
.

8.6.2
Preliminary Results

To obtain tighter bounds for this problem instance, instead of considering two separate rate-distortion functions as in Theorem
56
, we must instead analyze the joint
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion function. Let
\tilde{\Theta}_{M}
denote the set of random variables
\tilde{\theta}
such that for all
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
m\in\{1,2,\ldots,M\}
,
X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{\theta}|(\theta_{1:M},\psi,H_{m,t})
. Then let

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})\ =\ \inf_{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,M,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\psi,\theta_{1:M};\tilde{\theta}),

where

\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,M,T}\ =\ \left\{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{M}:\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,\theta_{1:m},H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\theta},H_{m,t}))\right]\leq\epsilon\right\}.

We now present the following error bounds in terms of the joint
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion function.

Theorem 60
.

(joint
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion error bound)

For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]\ \leq\ \inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})}{MT}+\epsilon,

and

\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]\ \geq\ \sup_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})}{MT},\ \epsilon\right\}.

Proof.

We begin by establishing the upper bound.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:M})}{MT}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,M,T}}\ \frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:M},\tilde{\theta})}{MT}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,M,T}}\ \frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\tilde{\theta})}{MT}+\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\theta_{1:M}|\tilde{\theta})}{MT}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})}{MT}+\epsilon.

We now establish the lower bound. Suppose that
\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:M})<\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})
. Let
\tilde{\theta}=\tilde{H}_{M,T}\notin\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,M,T}
where
\tilde{H}_{M,T}
is another independent history sampled in the same manner as
H_{M,T}
.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:M})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T})-\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T}|\psi,\theta_{1:M})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T})-\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T}|\psi,\theta_{1:M},\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T}|\tilde{\theta})-\mathbb{H}(H_{M,T}|\psi,\theta_{1:M},\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:M}|\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\geq}\epsilon MT

where
(a)
follows from conditional independence assumptions,
(b)
follows from the fact that conditioning reduces entropy, and
c)
follows from the fact that
\tilde{\theta}\notin\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,M,T}
. Therefore, for all
\epsilon\geq 0,\ \mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:M})\geq\min\{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M}),\epsilon MT\}
. The result follows.
∎

Using the above combined rate-distortion result instead of one that separates meta parameter versus intra-task rate-distortion functions (Theorem
56
) will allow us to dramatically tighten our error bound. This is because it suffices to learn about models that generate significant numbers of documents.

Consider a random variable
\tilde{\psi}
which approximates
\psi
but additionally identifies which transformer models
actually
generated the documents. Recall that
\tilde{\Psi}
denotes the set of random variables over which we minimize to attain the meta parameter rate-distortion function. Note that
\tilde{\psi}\notin\tilde{\Psi}
because
\tilde{\psi}
is not independent of
H_{M,T}
, even when conditioned on
\psi
. However,
\tilde{\psi}
is independent of
H_{M,T}
when conditioned on
(\psi,\theta_{1:M})
, so
\tilde{\psi}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{M}
and thus can be used to bound the joint rate-distortion function.

In the following result, we establish an upper bound on the joint
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion function for the mixture of transformers problem.

Lemma 61
.

(mixture of transformers joint
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion upper bound)

For all
d,r,K,L,M,T
, if for all
(m,t)\in[M]\times[T]
,
X^{(m)}_{t}
is generated by the mixture of transformers process, then

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})\ \leq\ M\ln(N)+R\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\cdot r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(1+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{\epsilon}\right).

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\theta}=(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{M})
and let
\tilde{\psi}=(\tilde{\psi}_{j}:j\in\{1,2,\ldots,N\})
, where

\tilde{\psi}_{j}=\begin{cases}\left(\tilde{V}_{j,\ell},\tilde{A}_{j,\ell}:\ell\in\{1,2,\ldots,L\}\right)&{\rm if\ }j\in\mathcal{I}_{M}\\
\emptyset&{\rm otherwise}\\
\end{cases},

\left(\tilde{V}_{j,\ell},\tilde{A}_{j,\ell}\right)=\left(V_{j,\ell}+Z_{j,\ell},\ A_{j,\ell}+B_{j,\ell}\right)

(Z_{j,\ell},B_{j,\ell})\perp(V_{j,\ell},A_{j,\ell}),\quad Z_{j,\ell}\overset{iid}{\sim}\begin{cases}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}/r^{2})&\text{ if }\ell<L\\
\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}/(rd)&\text{ if }\ell=L\\
\end{cases};\quad B_{j,\ell}\overset{iid}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}/r^{2}),

\mathcal{I}_{M}
denotes the set of unique outcomes in
(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{M})
, and
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon}{KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}
.

We begin by upper bounding the rate

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\left(\tilde{\psi},\tilde{\theta};\psi,\theta_{1:M}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}(\tilde{\theta};\psi,\theta_{1:M})+\mathbb{I}\left(\tilde{\psi};\psi,\theta_{1:M}|\tilde{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}(\tilde{\theta};\psi,\theta_{1:M})+\mathbb{I}\left(\tilde{\psi};\psi|\tilde{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{H}(\tilde{\theta})+\mathbb{I}\left(\tilde{\psi};\psi|\tilde{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq M\ln(N)+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbbm{1}_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{M}}\cdot\mathbb{I}(\tilde{\psi}_{i},\psi_{i})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}M\ln(N)+R\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\cdot r\cdot\max\{r,d\}L\ln\left(1+\frac{r\cdot\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{\epsilon}\right),

where
(a)
follows from Lemmas
43
and
52

We now upper bound the distortion:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\psi,\theta_{1:M}|\tilde{\theta})}{MT}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(H_{M,T};\theta_{1:M}|\tilde{\theta})}{MT}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{1:M}|\tilde{\theta},H_{m-1,T})}{MT}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(1)}_{0:T};\theta_{1:M}|\tilde{\theta})}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(1)}_{0:T};\theta_{1}|\tilde{\psi}_{i_{1}})}{T}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\epsilon,

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
52
.
∎

8.6.3
Main Result

Equipped with the rate-distortion results from the previous section, we establish the following upper bound on the error of the mixture of transformers process.

Theorem 62
.

(mixture of transformers error upper bound)

For all
d,r,L,K,R,N,M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, if for all
(m,t)\in[M]\times[T]
,
X^{(m)}_{t}
is generated according to the mixture of transformers process, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{M,T}\leq\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL^{2}\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\ln\left(8Ke(1+16K)\right)}{MT}+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\ln\left(\frac{2KMT^{2}}{L}\right)}{MT}+\frac{\ln(N)}{T}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\frac{\mathbb{H}_{M,T,\epsilon}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})}{MT}+\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\frac{\ln(N)}{T}+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\ln\left(1+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}KLT(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{\epsilon}\right)}{MT}+\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ln(N)}{T}+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\ln\left(1+\frac{KMT^{2}(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{L}\right)}{MT}+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL^{2}}{MT}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ln(N)}{T}+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\ln\left(\frac{2KMT^{2}(8K(1+16K))^{L}}{L}\right)}{MT}+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL^{2}}{MT}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL^{2}\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\ln\left(8Ke(1+16K)\right)}{MT}+\frac{r\max\{r,d\}RL\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{R}\right)\ln\left(\frac{2KMT^{2}}{L}\right)}{MT}+\frac{\ln(N)}{T}

∎

Notably, the term which decays linearly in
T
indicates that once the transformer weights of the sampled models are learned, the only information which must be deduced from each document is its index. As a result, the error grows logarithmically in
N
(the size of the mixture) and decays linearly in
T
(the length of the intra-task sequence). The first term scales linearly in the product of the parameter count and depth of the transformer model. Furthermore it also scales linearly in
R\ln(1+M/R)
, the expected number of unique mixture elements from
M
documents. This component of the error decays linearly in
MT
, so it will decay both as the number of documents and the number of tokens per document grow. As a result, for a pre-training dataset with sufficiently large
M
, this portion of the error could become negligible, which would indicate that the loss would eventually be dominated by
\ln(N)/T
. This is intuitive as once the transformer weights have been learned, the algorithm just has to disambiguate which index the next document belongs to.

Summary

•

Meta-learning consists of a random process
(X_{t}^{(m)}:m,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{++})
of observations. For any fixed
m
,
(X_{0}^{(m)},X_{1}^{(m)},\ldots)
represents the data associated with a particular
task
indexed by
m
.

•

The
meta-parameters

\psi
, represent information which is shared
across
tasks.

•

The
intra-task parameters

(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\ldots)
are iid when conditioned on
\psi
and for all
m
,
X_{0:\infty}^{(m)}\perp\psi|\theta_{m}
.

•

A meta-learning algorithm
\pi
produces for each
(m,t)\in[M]\times[T]
, a predictive distribution
P_{m,t}
of
X_{t+1}^{(m)}
after observing the concatenated history which we denote by

H_{m,t}=\left(X^{(1)}_{0:T},X^{(2)}_{0:T},\ldots,X^{(m-1)}_{0:T},X^{(m)}_{0:t}\right).

•

For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
, we measure the error realized by our predictions
P_{m,t}
with respect to the gold-standard clairvoyant prediction
P^{*}_{m,t}=\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,\theta_{1:M},H_{m,t})
via the expected KL divergence:

\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M-1}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|P_{m,t})\right].

•

(Bayesian posterior is optimal)

For all
m,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]=\min_{\pi}\ \mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|P_{m,t})\right].

•

(error decomposition)

For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|H_{m,t}))\right]}_{{\rm meta\ error}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta_{m},\psi,H_{m,t})\ \|\ \mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t}))\right]}_{{\rm intra-task\ error}}.

•

Let
\tilde{\Psi}
denote the set of random variables
\tilde{\psi}
such that for all
m,t
,
X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{\psi}|(\psi,H_{m,t})
. Then, let

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)\ =\ \inf_{\tilde{\psi}\in\tilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon,M,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\psi;\tilde{\psi}),

where

\tilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon,M,T}\ =\ \left\{\tilde{\psi}\in\tilde{\Psi}:\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\psi},H_{m,t}))\right]\leq\epsilon\right\}.

•

Let
\tilde{\Theta}_{m}
denote the set of random variables
\tilde{\theta}_{m}
such that for all
t
,
X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{\theta}_{m}|(\theta_{m},H_{m,t})
. Then, let

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)\ =\ \inf_{\tilde{\theta}_{m}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,m,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\theta_{m};\tilde{\theta}_{m}|\psi),

where

\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,m,T}\ =\ \left\{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{m}:\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{\mathbb{I}(X^{(m)}_{0:T};\theta_{m}|\tilde{\theta},\psi)}{T}\leq\epsilon\right\}.

•

(
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion error bound)

For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and
m\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle\ \leq\ \inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)}{MT}+\epsilon\ +\ \inf_{\epsilon^{\prime}\geq 0}\ \frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon^{\prime},T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)}{T}+\epsilon^{\prime},

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{m,t}\|\hat{P}_{m,t})\right]

\displaystyle\ \geq\ \sup_{\epsilon\geq 0}\min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi)}{MT},\epsilon\right\}\ +\ \sup_{\epsilon^{\prime}\geq 0}\min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon^{\prime},T}(\theta_{m}|\psi)}{T},\epsilon^{\prime}\right\}.

•

Let
\tilde{\Theta}_{M}
denote the set of random variables
\tilde{\theta}
such that for all
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
m\in\{1,2,\ldots,M\}
,

X^{(m)}_{t+1}\perp\tilde{\theta}|(\theta_{1:M},\psi,H_{m,t})
. Then let

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})\ =\ \inf_{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,M,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\psi,\theta_{1:M};\tilde{\theta}),

where

\tilde{\Theta}_{M,T,\epsilon}\ =\ \left\{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{M}:\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\psi,\theta_{1:m},H_{m,t})\|\mathbb{P}(X^{(m)}_{t+1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\theta},H_{m,t}))\right]\leq\epsilon\right\}.

•

(joint
(M,T)
-horizon rate-distortion error bound)

For all
M,T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]\ \leq\ \inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})}{MT}+\epsilon,

and

\frac{1}{MT}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{P}_{t})\right]\ \geq\ \sup_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,M,T}(\psi,\theta_{1:M})}{MT},\ \epsilon\right\}.

9
Misspecification

All results presented in previous chapters pertain to an agent that carries out Bayesian inference with a correctly specified prior distribution. In this chapter, we establish results that characterize the impact of prior misspecification. We apply these results to study error with misspecified linear and neural network models.

9.1
General Theoretical Results

In order to model the misspecified prior, we introduce an alternative probability measure
\mathbb{Q}
on
(\Omega,\mathbb{F})
. This measure
\mathbb{Q}
may assign a different marginal distribution to
\theta
but distributions conditioned on
\theta
are identical:
\mathbb{Q}(\cdot|\theta)=\mathbb{P}(\cdot|\theta)
. In particular, for all
t
,

\mathbb{Q}(H_{t}\in\cdot|\theta)=\mathbb{P}(H_{t}\in\cdot|\theta)
.
The posterior of
\theta
conditioned on
H_{t}=h
under the misspecified prior is
\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot|H_{t}=h)
. We will use the notation
\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot|H_{t}\leftarrow H_{t})
to express the random distribution obtained by sampling
h
from
\mathbb{P}(H_{t}\in\cdot)
and calculating the conditional probability
\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot|H_{t}=h)
.

For all
t
, let

\hat{Q}_{t}=\mathbb{Q}(Y_{t+1}\in\cdot|H_{t}\leftarrow H_{t}).

Note that this is the posterior predictive distribution with the history drawn according to
\mathbb{P}
and Bayesian inference carried out according to
\mathbb{Q}
. Our analysis will make use of a set
\Theta^{\dagger}
consisting of random variables
\theta^{\dagger}
for which
\mathbb{P}(\theta^{\dagger}\in\cdot)
and
\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot)
are equivalent measures; that is, each is absolutely continuous with respect to the other. We refer to such a random variable
\theta^{\dagger}
as a
constrained approximation
of
\theta
. When
\mathbb{P}(\theta\in\cdot)
and
\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot)
are equivalent measures, we will typically take the constrained approximation
\theta^{\dagger}
to be identical to
\theta
. Otherwise,
\theta^{\dagger}
will typically forgo some information expressed by
\theta
; the amount of information lost depends on the degree of model misspecification.

Recall that the
T
-horizon rate-distortion function of
\theta
is

\mathbb{H}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta)=\ \inf_{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\theta;\tilde{\theta}),

where

\tilde{\Theta}_{\epsilon,T}=\left\{\tilde{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}:\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(Y_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta,H_{t})\|\mathbb{P}(Y_{t+1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\theta},H_{t}))\right]\leq\epsilon\right\}.

Recall that
\tilde{\Theta}
is the set of random variables
\tilde{\theta}
such that, for all
t
,
\tilde{\theta}\perp Y_{t+1}|(\theta,H_{t})
. Recall that we interpret
\tilde{\theta}
as an (unconstrained) approximation of
\theta
. In this chapter, we will use a variation of this definition that pertains to approximation of
\theta^{\dagger}
instead of
\theta
. In particular, for any random variable
\theta^{\dagger}\in\Theta^{\dagger}
, let

\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta^{\dagger})=\ \inf_{\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger}\in\tilde{\Theta}^{\dagger}_{\epsilon,T}}\ \mathbb{I}(\theta^{\dagger};\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger}),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}^{\dagger}_{\epsilon,T}

\displaystyle=\left\{\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger}\in\tilde{\Theta}:\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(Y_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta^{\dagger},\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger},H_{t})\|\mathbb{P}(Y_{t+1}\in\cdot|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger},H_{t}))\right]\leq\epsilon\right\}.

The following result upper bounds the error of
\hat{Q}_{t}
in terms of an irreducible misspecification error and the
T
-horizon rate-distortion function.

Theorem 63
.

(misspecified learner error bound)

For all
T\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\ \mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}\left(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{Q}_{t}\right)\right]\leq\ \inf_{\theta^{\dagger}\in\Theta^{\dagger}}\left(\underbrace{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|P^{\dagger}_{t})\right]+\frac{{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}(\theta^{\dagger}\in\cdot)\|\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot))}{T}}_{\rm misspecification\ error}\ +\ \underbrace{\inf_{\epsilon\geq 0}\ \left(\frac{\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}_{\epsilon,T}(\theta^{\dagger})}{T}+\epsilon\right)}_{\rm statistical\ error}\right),

where
P^{\dagger}_{t}=\mathbb{P}(Y_{t+1}\in\cdot|\theta\leftarrow\theta^{\dagger},H_{t})
.

Proof.

While the stated result applies to continuous random variables, to keep this proof simple, we will restrict attention to the discrete case, leaving extension to the reader.

Fix some
\theta^{\dagger}\in\Theta^{\dagger}
and let
f
denote a random function such that
\mathbb{P}(f(\theta^{\dagger})\in\cdot)\overset{d}{=}\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot)
. Such a function
f
must exist since
\mathbb{P}(\theta^{\dagger}\in\cdot)
and
\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot)
are equivalent measures. Let
\Theta_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the support of
\mathbb{Q}(\theta\in\cdot)
and let

\mathbb{Q}(H_{T})=\sum_{\nu\in\Theta_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{Q}(\theta=\nu)\cdot\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta=\nu).

Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\ \mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{d}}_{\rm KL}(P^{*}_{t}\|\hat{Q}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(Y_{t+1}|\theta,H_{t}\right)}{\hat{Q}_{t}(Y_{t+1})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta)}{\mathbb{Q}(H_{T})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta)}{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}{\mathbb{Q}(H_{T})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta)}{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}{\sum_{\nu\in\Theta_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{Q}(\theta=\nu)\cdot\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta=\nu)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta)}{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}{\sum_{\nu\in\Theta_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{P}(f(\theta^{\dagger})=\nu)\cdot\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta=\nu)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta)}{\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\sum_{\nu\in\Theta_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{P}(H_{T},\theta^{\dagger}=\nu|\tilde{\theta}^{\dagger})}{\sum_{\nu\in\Theta_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{P}(f(\theta^{\dagger})=\nu)\cdot\mathbb{P}(H_{T}|\theta=\nu)}\right]
